L2124: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2125: Higher talquetamab Cavg and Ctrough were associated with better ORR according to an Emax model in MonumentAL-1 patients with RRMM. The efficacy reached a plateau at higher exposures. The E-R efficacy analysis supports the 0.4 mg/kg QW regimen and the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen.
L2126: **Applicant Figure 19 and Applicant Figure 20** summarize the Applicant's E-R efficacy analysis in 134 MonumentAL-1 Phase 1 patients with RRMM who received any SC dosage of talquetamab (111 patients without prior CAR-T/BsAb therapy plus 23 patients with prior CAR-T/BsAb therapy). The Phase 1 data suggest that ORR improves with higher exposure (Cavg,4wks and Ctrough,FD1) up to a plateau of efficacy which occurs below the median exposure for either RP2D (i.e., 0.4 mg/kg QW or 0.8 mg/kg Q2W), as shown in **Applicant Figure 19**. Although the lowest exposure quartile appears to have the worst observed progression-free survival (PFS) in **Applicant Figure 20**, the confidence intervals overlap across exposure quartiles and the Phase 1 data does not show a clear E-R association with PFS or duration of response (DOR).
L2127: **Applicant Figure 21, Applicant Table 28, Applicant Figure 23, and Applicant Table 29** summarize the Applicant's E-R efficacy analysis in 242 MonumentAL-1 Phase 1 + Phase 2 patients with RRMM treated with either RP2D who had at least 12 weeks of data at the clinical cutoff date of 16 May 2022 and no prior use of CAR-T or BsAb therapy. Patients with prior CAR-T or BsAb therapy provide supportive efficacy data (refer to **Section 8.1.2**) and were included in the Applicant's Phase 1 E-R efficacy analysis. Therefore, FDA conducted additional analysis to evaluate E-R associations in MonumentAL-1 Phase 1 + Phase 2 patients who received any SC talquetamab dose, regardless of prior CAR-T/BsAb therapy status.
L2128: The FDA evaluated potential E-R associations with PFS, OS, and DOR in 386 patients with PK and efficacy data, and E-R associations with ORR in 354 patients who had at least 12 weeks of PK and efficacy data at the clinical cutoff date. A total of 75/386 (19%) patients in the PFS/OS/DOR analysis and 70/354 (20%) patients in the ORR analysis and had received prior CAR-T/BsAb therapy.
L2129: FDA reviewer analysis found that higher Ctrough,FD1 was associated with better ORR according to an Emax model, as shown in **FDA Figure 17**. ORR appeared to plateau at a value close to the
L2130: median C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} for both proposed regimens. Similar to the Applicant's analysis of Phase 1 patients in Applicant Figure 19, the Emax model of ORR according to C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} (FDA Figure 17) appeared to fit the observed ORR data better than C_{\text{avg},4\text{wks}} or average concentration following first treatment dose (C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1}). The Kaplan-Meier plots of OS versus exposure quartiles in FDA Figure 18 suggest that the highest three exposure quartiles of C_{\text{avg},4\text{wks}}, C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1}, and C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} were associated with better OS compared to the lowest exposure quartile.
L2131: **FDA Figure 17: Exposure-Response Emax Model of ORR Versus C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} in MonumentAL-1 Patients with RRMM**
L2132: C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} was simulated in 354 patients with RRMM in MonumentAL-1 who received at least one SC dose of talquetamab with any dosing regimen and who had at least 12 weeks of data at the clinical cutoff date. Emax model summarizes data in 350/354 patients with both C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} and ORR data. Boxplots summarize simulated C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} in 181 patients who received the 0.4 mg/kg QW regimen and 114 patients who received the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen. Vertical reference lines represent median C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} in patients who received the 0.4 mg/kg QW regimen (pink) and patients who received the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen (blue).

L2134: ## FDA Figure 18: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Overall Survival versus Exposure Quartiles
L2135: (a) Overall Survival versus C_{avg,4wks} Quartile

L2138: Exposure was simulated in 386 patients with RRMM in MonumentAL-1 who received at least one dose of subcutaneous talquetamab with any dosing regimen.
L2139: C_{\text{avg},4\text{wks}} = average concentration over the first four weeks of treatment doses; C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1} = average concentration following first treatment dose; C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1} = average concentration over the first four weeks of treatment doses; OS = overall survival; Q = quartile (e.g., Q1 = 1st quartile); RRMM = relapsed/refractory multiple myeloma.
L2140: Figure source: Reviewer analysis.
L2141: Multivariate Cox proportional hazard modeling was performed to evaluate E-R associations after accounting for potential covariate effects on efficacy. C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1} had a stronger association with OS compared to C_{\text{avg},4\text{wks}} or C_{\text{trough},\text{FD}1}, and lower C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1} was still associated with worse OS after including other significant covariate effects. The final Cox proportional hazard model identified statistically significant associations between better OS and C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1} above 513 ng/mL (i.e., greater than the 25th percentile of C_{\text{avg},\text{FD}1}, yes versus no), lower ALP, lower LDH, ECOG score equal to 0 (versus 1 or 2), and no high disease burden at baseline. However, interpretation of OS results are limited by the single-arm trial design. The final Cox proportional hazard model of OS is summarized in FDA Table 37. Of note, prior CAR-T/BsAb therapy status was not associated with OS after accounting for statistically significant covariates in the final model.

L2147: FDA review did not identify any clear E-R associations with PFS or DOR across Cavg,4wks or Ctrough,FD1 quartiles, which is congruent with the Applicant's analyses in **Applicant Figure 20** and **Applicant Figure 23**.
L2148: Overall, E-R efficacy analysis shows that higher talquetamab exposure was generally associated with better ORR in MonumenTAL-1 patients with RRMM and that the ORR plateaus at an exposure less than or equal to the median exposures following 0.4 mg/kg QW and 0.8 mg/kg Q2W. The E-R efficacy analysis supports both proposed dosage regimens (i.e., 0.4 mg/kg QW and 0.8 mg/kg Q2W).
L2149: ## 19.4.2.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2150: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2151: The E-R safety analysis supports both the 0.4 mg/kg QW regimen and the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen.
L2152: Higher talquetamab exposures (i.e., Cavg and Cmax) are associated with higher incidence of dysgeusia, Grade ≥2 dysgeusia, skin toxicity, Grade ≥2 skin toxicity, skin exfoliation, nail toxicity, oral toxicity, and Grade ≥2 oral toxicity.
L2153: After accounting for the difference in median treatment duration between regimens in Study MonumenTAL-1, the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen had a higher risk of Grade 1 skin toxicity and Grade 1 skin exfoliation compared to 0.4 mg/kg QW which may be due to the higher Cavg or Cmax with the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen. However, E-R safety analysis did not identify any major safety differences between the proposed regimens.
L2154: ## 19.4.2.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L2155: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2156: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To explore the E-R relationship for selected treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs)

L2157: Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
Model Structure	Quartile analysis	Higher talquetamab exposures (i.e., C_{avg} and C_{max}) were associated with higher incidence of dysgeusia, Grade \ge 2 dysgeusia, skin toxicity, Grade \ge 2 skin toxicity, skin exfoliation, nail toxicity, oral toxicity, and Grade \ge 2 oral toxicity.

After accounting for the difference in median treatment duration between regimens in Study MonumentAL-1, the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen had a higher risk of Grade 1 skin toxicity and Grade 1 skin exfoliation compared to 0.4 mg/kg QW which may be due to the higher steady state C_{max} with the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen.
Model Parameter Estimates	Summary of selected TEAEs of safety endpoints rates and 95% CI overall and stratified by exposure quartiles are presented in Applicant Table 32, Applicant Table 33 and Applicant Table 34.
CRS endpoints by exposure quartiles were summarized in Applicant Table 35.

Model Evaluation	There were no observed E-R relationships across the examined safety endpoints including Grade \ge 3 infections, baseline worsening Grade \ge 3 cytopenia (thrombocytopenia, anemia, neutropenia, leukopenia, and lymphopenia); or Grade \ge 2 ageusia/weight loss and TEAE leading to dose modifications across the talquetamab exposure quartiles. In addition, Grade \ge 3 rash, Grade \ge 3 hypogammaglobulinemia, Grade \ge 3 tumor lysis, Grade \ge 3 neurotoxicity, Grade \ge 3 CRS, and Grade \ge 2 nail loss had very low incidences and were not feasible for E-R analysis.
There were no observed E-R relationships for CRS (Grade \ge 1, Grade\ge 2) and peak exposure of talquetamab for any step-up doses, regardless of tocilizumab administration or not.
An E-R relationship was observed in the rate of Grade 2 dysgeusia and any grade dysgeusia. Dysgeusia incidence rates were comparable between the two RP2D doses.

Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Covariates including the pharmacokinetics covariates of type of myeloma and ISS stage were evaluated and found to have no impact on dysgeusia any grade and Grade \ge 2 (Applicant Figure 27).
There were no observed E-R relationships for CRS (Grade \ge 1, Grade\ge 2) and peak exposure of talquetamab for any step-up doses, regardless of tocilizumab administration or not (Applicant Figure 30, Applicant Figure 31, and Applicant Figure 32).

Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable	
Visualization of E-R relationships	Applicant Figure 24, Applicant Figure 25, Applicant Figure 26, and Applicant Figure 28 for quartile analyses of selected safety endpoints;
Applicant Figure 29 for CRS E-R quartile analysis;
TEAE incidence according to exposure quartiles are presented for skin toxicity in FDA Figure 19, skin exfoliation in FDA Figure
L2158: Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Applicant Table 31 for baseline characteristics stratified by exposure quartiles.
20, nail toxicity in FDA Figure 21, and oral toxicity in FDA Figure 22.
The time-to-first-event for each exposure quartile is presented for dysgeusia in FDA Figure 23, skin toxicity in FDA Figure 24, skin exfoliation in FDA Figure 25, nail toxicity in FDA Figure 26, and oral toxicity in FDA Figure 27.

Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	
No positive ER safety trend was apparent for selected safety endpoints including CRS, except for dysgeusia. However, dysgeusia incidence rates were comparable between the two RP2D doses.
Higher talquetamab exposure was associated with multiple TEAEs. The probability of Grade 1 skin toxicity and Grade 1 skin exfoliation was higher with 0.8 mg/kg Q2W compared to 0.4 mg/kg QW, which may be due to the higher C_{max} following the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen. E-R safety analysis did not identify any major safety differences between the proposed regimens.

L2221: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2222: Higher talquetamab exposure was associated with multiple TEAEs. Out of the two proposed dosage regimens (i.e., 0.4 mg/kg QW and 0.8 mg/kg Q2W), the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen has a higher probability of Grade 1 skin toxicity and Grade 1 skin exfoliation which may be due to the higher C_{avg} or C_{max} following 0.8 mg/kg Q2W. The E-R safety analysis did not identify a major safety difference between the two proposed regimens, and both proposed regimens are supported by the E-R safety analysis.
L2223: The E-R safety population (n=353 total) consisted of MonumentAL-1 Phase 1 patients with RRMM (n=132) and Phase 2 patients with RRMM and no prior use of CAR-T or BsAb therapy (n=221) who received at least 1 dose of SC talquetamab.
L2224: Higher exposure (C_{max} and C_{avg}) was associated with higher incidence of dysgeusia and Grade \ge 2 dysgeusia, as shown in **Applicant Table 34** and **Applicant Figure 26**. Higher exposure was also associated with higher incidence of skin toxicity and Grade \ge 2 skin toxicity (**FDA Figure 19**); skin exfoliation (**FDA Figure 20**); nail toxicity (**FDA Figure 21**); and oral toxicity and Grade \ge 2 oral toxicity (**FDA Figure 22**).

L2249: AE = adverse event; CAVG4WKS = predicted average concentration over the first 4 weeks of treatment full doses (i.e., Cavg,4wks); CI = confidence interval; CMAXFD1 = predicted maximum concentration after the first treatment full dose (i.e., Cmax,FD1); CMAX4WKS = predicted maximum concentration over the first 4 weeks of treatment full doses (i.e., Cmax,4wks); +G2 = Grade \ge2; n = number of participants.
L2250: Source: Figure 25 and Figure 26 in Applicant's Response to 3 Feb 2023 IR, dated 13 Feb 2023.
L2251: Out of 353 total patients in the E-R safety analysis, the number of patients who received the proposed 0.4 mg/kg QW and 0.8 mg/kg Q2W dosages were similar (n=148 versus n=146). However, due to the protocol timeline, patients who received 0.4 mg/kg QW tended to have longer durations of treatment and follow-up compared to patients who received 0.8 mg/kg Q2W. The median treatment duration was 6.9 months for 0.4 mg/kg QW and 3.7 months for 0.8 mg/kg Q2W, while the median follow-up duration was 11.0 months for 0.4 mg/kg QW and 5.1 months for 0.8 mg/kg Q2W (refer to **Section 8.2.2** for details).
L2252: The differences in follow-up duration may confound associations between TEAE incidence and exposure, since 0.8 mg/kg Q2W resulted in higher Cavg and Cmax values compared to 0.4 mg/kg QW (refer to **Section 19.4.1.2**). Therefore, FDA compared time to first event across exposure quartiles and across regimens for select TEAEs.
L2253: After accounting for differences in follow-up duration, higher talquetamab exposures (C_{\text{avg},4\text{wks}} and C_{\text{max},4\text{wks}}) were still associated with higher rates of dysgeusia and Grade \ge 2 dysgeusia (FDA Figure 23); skin toxicity and Grade \ge 2 skin toxicity (FDA Figure 24); skin exfoliation (FDA Figure 25); nail toxicity (FDA Figure 26); and Grade \ge 2 oral toxicity (FDA Figure 27). First incidence of oral toxicity also tended to occur earlier with higher talquetamab exposure (FDA Figure 27).

L2270: Patients who received 0.4 mg/kg QW and 0.8 mg/kg Q2W did not appear to have a significant difference in the time to first dysgeusia, Grade \ge 2 dysgeusia, Grade \ge 2 skin toxicity, nail toxicity, oral toxicity, or Grade \ge 2 oral toxicity, (FDA Figure 28, FDA Figure 29, FDA Figure 30, and FDA Figure 31). However, FDA Figure 29 and FDA Figure 32 show that risk of skin toxicity and skin exfoliation were higher with the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen compared to the 0.4 mg/kg regimen after accounting for the difference in median treatment duration. The risk difference between
L2271: regimens was driven by Grade 1 events, and so the higher risk of skin toxicity and skin exfoliation does not warrant a major safety concern for the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen. The E-R safety analysis supports both the 0.4 mg/kg QW regimen and the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W regimen.

L2290: ## 19.4.2.5. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L2291: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2292: The E-R analyses provide the scientific and quantitative justification for the selected SC doses of talquetamab, ie, 0.4 mg weekly SC or 0.8 mg Q2W SC, to treat adults with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma.
L2293: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2294: The E-R analyses of efficacy (Section 19.4.2.2) and safety (Section 19.4.2.4) support the 0.4 mg/kg SC QW and 0.8 mg/kg SC Q2W dosage regimens for the treatment of patients with RRMM.
L2295: ## 19.4.3. Physiologically based PK Analysis
L2296: (b) (4)
L2297: The sponsor has developed a PBPK model
L2298: The model development and validation are included in the Report FK14333 (BLA 761342, Module 5.3.5.4).
L2299: (b) (4)
L2300: (b) (4)
L2301: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2302: The Reviewer considered the PBPK analyses inadequate to evaluate the drug interaction potential of talquetamab and duration of its effect on CYP substrate drugs because an exposure-response relationship between plasma IL-6 levels and changes in CYP activity in vivo has not been established. Detailed rationale of the limitations of the PBPK analyses follows below:
L2303: ## **1. Uncertainty about in-vitro-in-vivo extrapolation of IL-6 suppression data**
L2304: (b) (4)
L2305: 2 Page(s) has been Withheld in Full as b4 (CCI/TS) immediately following this page
L2306: (b) (4)
L2307: ## 2. *The ability of PBPK analysis to predict the time course of IL-6 effect on CYP activity has not been demonstrated*
L2308: To date, the time course of the effects of transient increased IL-6 levels, caused by therapeutic protein treatment, on CYP activity has not been studied in patients. Therefore, the ability of PBPK analysis to predict the time course of the effect of IL-6 on CYP activity has not been demonstrated.
L2309: (b) (4)
L2310: (b) (4)
L2311: (b) (4)
L2312: The Applicant's PBPK modeling did not seem to capture the time course of suppression/induction and recovery from the IL-6-mediated CYP modulation effect triggered by talquetamab administration.
L2313: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L2314: 342
L2315: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L2316: 2 Page(s) has been Withheld in Full as b4 (CCI/TS) immediately following this page
L2317: The Applicant proposal of monitoring period for drug interaction risk – from initiation of talquetamab step-up phase (b) (4) the first treatment dose – may be insufficient for sensitive CYP substrate drugs with narrow therapeutic index. We recommend monitoring from initiation of talquetamab step-up phase and at least 2 weeks after the first treatment dose, and during and after a CRS event.
L2318: ## ***Model Assessment Criteria***
L2319: ***Context of use:*** PBPK modeling analysis will be used to assess the impact of transient increase of plasma IL-6 levels, following the step-up dosing phase of talquetamab, on the PK of concomitant CYP substrate drugs. Modeling data are intended to be the basis to support labeling recommendations on clinical monitoring of CYP substrate drugs with narrow therapeutic index during the events of cytokine release syndrome.
L2320: ***Model influence:*** High. Modeling and simulation data will be used in lieu of a clinical data with a CYP substrate drug. Thus, the modeling analysis would provide substantial evidence when no clinical data relevant to the context of use is available to inform the decision.
L2321: ***Decision consequence:*** Medium. A possible increase in exposure to concomitant medications that are CYP substrates with narrow therapeutic index may increase the risk of adverse events.
L2322: ***Model risk:*** Medium, as model influence is high and decision consequence is medium.
L2323: ## ***Conclusions***
L2324: The Reviewer considered the Applicant's PBPK modeling analysis inadequate because exposure-response relationship between plasma IL-6 levels and CYP activity has not been established in humans. Therefore, the analysis cannot be used as primary basis to support labeling recommendations about clinical monitoring of co-medications.
L2325: The Applicant proposed the following labeling related to drug interaction liability of talquetamab: (b) (4)
L2326: (b) (4)
L2327: Based on Reviewer's evaluation of available clinical data in the literature and understanding of postulated mechanism of IL-6 mediated CYP suppression and estimated time of CYP recovery, we recommend monitoring from initiation of talquetamab step-up phase and at least 2 weeks after the first treatment dose, and during and after CRS.

L2370: Figure 11 displays two boxplots comparing Talquetamab Ctrough concentration (Y-axis) across Visit Time (X-axis: Cycle 01 Day 1, Cycle 02 Day 1, Cycle 03 Day 1, Cycle 04 Day 1, Cycle 06 Day 1, Cycle 07 Day 1) for Cohort A (left) and Cohort C (right), stratified by Anti-Talquetamab Antibody (ADA) Status (Negative/Positive).
Cohort A (Boxplot): Concentration ranges from 0 to 8000. The median Ctrough for Negative ADA status (blue boxes) generally increases over time, reaching approximately 3000-4000. The median Ctrough for Positive ADA status (red dashed line with diamond markers) is lower, starting around 1000 and decreasing slightly over time. A statistically significant difference (*) is noted between Negative and Positive ADA status at Cycle 01 Day 1.
Cohort C (Boxplot): Concentration ranges from 0 to 6000. The median Ctrough for Negative ADA status (blue boxes) generally increases over time, reaching approximately 2000-3000. The median Ctrough for Positive ADA status (red dashed line with diamond markers) is lower, starting around 1000 and increasing slightly over time. A statistically significant difference (*) is noted between Negative and Positive ADA status at Cycle 01 Day 1.
ADA Status: Negative (Blue Box), Positive (Red Dashed Line with Diamond Marker).
L2371: Figure 12 is a scatter plot showing the Geometric Mean Fold Change from Baseline (Y-axis) versus Visit and Time (X-axis) for three cytokines: Interleukin 2 Receptor (\text{pg}/\text{mL}), Interleukin 6 (\text{pg}/\text{mL}), and Interleukin 10 (\text{pg}/\text{mL}). The X-axis includes time points for PRIMING DOSE VISIT 1, PRIMING DOSE VISIT 2, and Cycles 01 Day 1 through Cycle 01 Day 15, with specific time points marked (0H, 2H, 6H, 24H, PREDOSE, 48H, 72H).
The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 12 for all three cytokines.
Interleukin 2 Receptor (Top Panel): The fold change generally remains stable around 1.5 to 2.0 across all visits and time points.
Interleukin 6 (Middle Panel): The fold change shows significant variability. It peaks around 10-12 at 24H and 48H during Cycle 01 Day 1, and peaks around 6-8 at 24H and 48H during Cycle 01 Day 2. It generally remains below 4 for most other time points.
Interleukin 10 (Bottom Panel): The fold change generally remains low, mostly below 2, with a peak around 2.5 at 24H during Cycle 01 Day 1.
L2372: Figure 13 is a scatter plot illustrating the Geometric Mean Fold Change from Baseline for Interleukin 2 Receptor (IL-2R), Interleukin 6 (IL-6), and Interleukin 10 (IL-10) concentrations over time in the 0.8 mg/kg Q2W cohort. The Y-axis represents the Geometric Mean Fold Change from Baseline, ranging from 0 to 15. The X-axis represents Visit and Time, including PRIMING DOSE VISIT 1, PRIMING DOSE VISIT 2, PRIMING DOSE VISIT 3, CYCLE 01 DAY 1, CYCLE 01 DAY 2, CYCLE 01 DAY 3, CYCLE 01 DAY 4, and CYCLE 01 DAY 15, with time points marked at 0H, 2H, 6H, 24H, 48H, and PREDOSE.
The data shows that IL-2R concentrations (top panel) generally remain stable near baseline (1.0 fold change). IL-6 concentrations (middle panel) show a significant increase, peaking around 12-13 fold change at 24H and 48H after the third priming dose (PRIMING DOSE VISIT 3). IL-10 concentrations (bottom panel) also show a peak, reaching approximately 4-5 fold change around 24H and 48H after the third priming dose.
L2373: Flowchart illustrating the All Treated Analysis Sets (N=501) for Study 64007654MMY1001.
The total population (N=501) is divided into four main groups:
Phase 1 Dose Escalation + Dose Expansion: This group is further divided into IV Cohorts (n=102) and SC Cohorts (n=134). The combined Phase 1 Dose-Finding Population (n=236) is analyzed for Weekly RP2D in Phase 1 (n=21) and Q2W RP2D in Phase 1 (n=36).
Phase 2 Weekly RP2D (No prior T cell redirection): This group includes 0.4 mg/kg weekly SC (n=122). This group is analyzed for Weekly RP2D in Phase 1 (n=21) and Q2W RP2D in Phase 1 (n=36).
Phase 2 Q2W RP2D (No prior T cell redirection): This group includes 0.8 mg/kg Q2W SC (n=109). This group is analyzed for Q2W RP2D in Phase 1 (n=36).
Phase 2 Weekly or Q2W RP2D (Prior T cell redirection): This group includes 0.4 mg/kg weekly SC or 0.8 mg/kg Q2W SC (n=34). This group is analyzed for Either RP2D and prior T cell redirection in Phase 1 (n=17).
The analysis sets are combined as follows:
Weekly RP2D in Phase 1 (n=21) is combined with Q2W RP2D in Phase 1 (n=36).
Q2W RP2D in Phase 1 (n=36) is combined with Either RP2D and prior T cell redirection in Phase 1 (n=17).

Legend: RP2D 400 µg/kg QW, RP2D 800 µg/kg Q2W.

Two dosage regimens are compared: RP2D 400 µg/kg QW (red/pink) and RP2D 800 µg/kg Q2W (teal/blue). The RP2D 800 µg/kg Q2W regimen shows significantly higher peak concentrations and overall exposure compared to the RP2D 400 µg/kg QW regimen. A dashed line indicates the Max EC90 (353 ng/mL).
